T4.1. EVOLVE2CARE Dissemination, Communication and Outreach for the Open Calls [M1-M24]
Leader: VILABS | Partners involved: ALL
With the aim to raise awareness and maximise the visibility of the project and its outcomes, a detailed plan will be
developed on M3 and updated throughout the project to coordinate the overall relevant activities. An initial structure
of the plan and its content (including strategy, goals, objectives, activities, targets, guidelines, material, etc.) is foreseen
as described in Section 2.2. Several well-tailored measures are: (i) development of the EVOLVE2CARE identity (e.g.,
logo, graphic design package) and materials (e.g., templates, leaflets, brochures etc.) (M2), (ii) development of the web
portal (M2), (iii) establishment of active social media presence (M2), (iv) promotional videos (M3 and every 3 months),
(v) monthly newsletters, ad-hoc press releases and publications in scientific journals and the “Open Research Europe”
Platform, following and promoting Open Science, and (vi) participation in external national and EU level conferences
and (online and physical) events that are of high interest and relevance. Available European Commission Tools, such
as ‘Horizon Magazine”, “Open Research Europe”, the “Horizon Results Booster” and “Horizon Results Platform”, will
accompany the activities of this task and support the effective dissemination of the EVOLVE2CARE. T4.1 will also
support the outreach of the EVOLVE2CARE open calls, trainings, knowledge sharing sessions, and final event. For that
reason, early in the project EVOLVE2CARE consortium will set up and sustain an active community of stakeholders
that will support the project’s activities and will potentially get involved in the open calls. The proper engagement of
stakeholders to the open calls will be secured as: (a) it will be based on the existing, wide networks, projects and clusters,
that the consortium partners are involved; and (b) tailored messages will be formulated for each stakeholder category.
T4.1 outcomes will be reported by VILABS in D4.1 due by M3 and updated by M24.
T4.2. Exploitation and IPR of AccelUP [M1-M24] Task leader: VILABS | Partners involved: ALL
T4.2 aims to ensure the exploitation, sustainability, and impact of EVOLVE2CARE results and stimulate their adoption
and effective uptake across Europe and beyond. The first action is the development of an exploitation strategy and a
plan to further utilise the projects’ tangible and intangible Key Exploitable Results (AccelUP) and the use of knowledge
produced, along with relevant IPR provisions. AccelUP exploitation plan and sustainable business models (T3.3) will
be co-designed, along with Business, Finance and Market Access plans. All partners will contribute to this task and the
EVOLVE2CARE stakeholders’ community will validate the applicability and relevance of the overall work (through
webinars and interviews). This task will also implement innovation management activities, towards ensuring that an
effective innovation cycle is in place to support the development of an innovative services/product. The work done under
Task 4.2 will be reported in D4.2 due by M24.
T4.3. Clustering with EIT KICs [M12-M24] | Leader: ENOLL | Partners involved: ALL
Since the transitional care sector is interdisciplinary, EVOLVE2CARE will engage with EIT KICs, including Health,
Mobility, and Digital, all of which have direct bearing on our 3 Use Cases a) hospital discharge management, b) home
care monitoring, and c) aging population and care transitions) EIT KICs are ideal sources to understand the impact of
regulation on the commercialization and innovation process, as they include a wide range of involved beneficiaries. In
addition, many of their stakeholders could benefit from the EVOLVE2CARE framework and activities. We will seek
to develop a strategic relationship with the various levels of management, within individual programs (such as RIS)
and across services, in order to a) familiarize them and their beneficiaries with the full spectrum of LL capabilities, b)
encourage their involvement with the AccelUp platform, c) leverage their knowledge regarding the regulatory continuum
in each sector. ENoLL will engage in detailed stakeholder research within the EIT KICs and the individual projects
– soliciting input from startups, researchers, administrators, and other involved parties such as mentors and service
providers - regarding the regulatory landscape and how it impacts our selected use cases of transitional care innovation
in each sector. For example, a digital solution for the monitoring and management of asthma is potentially affected
by regulatory developments in areas such as Health, Climate, Data Protection, and Smart Cities. EIT KICs are ideal
sources to provide the perspectives necessary for a complete understanding of the regulatory picture. T4.3 will include
an analytical report, produced by ENOLL and drawing on all the research and feedback that is carried out in the project,
identifying and comparing regulatory issues in each sector as they relate to transitional care innovation. This report
will be disseminated to all stakeholder groups, including KICs and associated projects, at a final event, organized by
ENOLL, with the aim of assisting policymakers at a pan-European level. T4.3 outcomes will be reported by ENOLL
in D4.3 due by M24.

| T5.1. Project Coordination and financial management [M1-M24] \| Leader: AUTH \| Partners involved: ALL. AUTH will assure that the orientation of the consortium activities matches the vision of EVOLVE2CARE. Partners will receive a project management handbook (PMH) that includes guidelines and templates for financial reporting, standards for deliverables and reports to the EC, steps to ensure timely reporting, a plan and calendar for payment procedures, etc., to effectively support project management. Internal communication procedures will also be established. Every 6 months (M7, M13), an interim summary report will be elaborated to keep on track the project activities & progress, WP, tasks and milestones status, project expenditures, update on project KPIs and lessons learned of respective period (with respective proposals for improvement). Deviations and corrective actions will also be reported. A specialized financial reporting tool called "Kronis" will be provided to all partners, by Sploro, for transparent and efficient financial management. Kronis will allow for real-time budget tracking, expenditure categorization, and automated report generation, ensuring that the project remains financially accountable and compliant with EU regulations. Every 6 months, an interim summary report will be elaborated to keep on track the project activities & progress, WP, tasks and milestones status, project expenditures, update on project KPIs and lessons learned of respective period (with respective proposals for improvement). Deviations and corrective actions will also be reported. Interim Reports will be delivered by AUTH in D5.2. AUTH will deliver the PMH in D5.1 due by M4 (initial version) and M24 (final version). T5.2. Technical, and Innovation Management [M1-M24] \| Leader: AV \| Partners involved: ALL T5.2 will perform technical and experimentation coordination to ensure that any technological advancements in the experimentation of products in the transitional care sector within EVOLVE2CARE, are aligned with evolving market needs. An external Advisory Board will be also established early during the project (M6) – initial high profile members are already onboarded (see 3.2.1.2), to facilitate an interdisciplinary support and advising channel regarding a) the utilization of various experimentation spaces i.e., Living Labs through the AccelUP platform, b) the acceleration of research iterations based on real-life documentation, and c) the systematic diffusion of results to the relevant policy makers and regulatory bodies. The Advisory Board will include members from the research community, the LLs, regulatory bodies, and corporates. AV will continuously monitor the experiments taking place under the various Use Cases, and will also standardize agreements regarding intellectual property rights (feeding T4.2), ensuring that innovators and researchers retain ownership of their innovations and with an eye on maintaining project high innovation potential of research results and greater impact. This task will be also responsible for creating the Ethics Advisory Board with at least 50% external experts. AUTH will also identify and contract an external Ethics Expert that will perform the final ethics check for the shortlisted projects. AV will report T5.2 outcomes in D5.3 due in M24. T5.3. Quality, Risk, and Data Management Plan [M1-M24] Leader: Sploro \| Partners involved: ALL Led by Sploro in collaboration with all project partners, this task aims to establish a robust framework for quality assurance, risk management and a data management framework. To maintain the highest quality standards, the project will develop and implement operating procedures and quality guidelines that will be disseminated to all partners. Regular audits and quality checks will be conducted to ensure compliance with these standards and to identify areas for improvement. Deliverables and completion of milestones will be monitored. A comprehensive risk assessment will be carried out at the outset and at various stages throughout the project. This will involve identifying potential risks related to project execution, financial management, and compliance, among others. Contingency plans will be developed for each identified risk, and a risk register will be maintained and regularly updated. T5.3 will analyze the main elements of the data management policy to be used throughout the EVOLVE2CARE project by the project partners, regarding all datasets generated by the project. A Data Management Plan (DMP) will be elaborated considering the “Guidelines on FAIR Data Management in H2020”, and will be a live document. By integrating these elements into a cohesive Quality, Risk and Data Management strategy, the project aims to ensure a structured, accountable, and transparent framework |

## Page 85

Project: 101158152 — EVOLVE2CARE — HORIZON-EIE-2023-CONNECT-02
Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3508396 - 15/05/2024
26

### Table 1

| Critical risks & risk management strategy Grant Preparation (Critical Risks screen) — Enter the info. | Column 2 | Column 3 | Column 4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Risk number | Description | Work Package No(s) | Proposed Mitigation Measures |
| 4 | Difficulties to find evaluators | WP2 | The partners have access to a solid network of professionals from prior projects. A few already participate in the external advisory board; others will be welcomed. |
| 5 | Solutions need to be scalable and replicable | WP2 | To evaluate and guarantee the potential for scalability and replication of the EVOLVE2CARE we will use specialized tools and procedures. Support, such as workshops and webinars, will be used to educate participants about the advantages of the EVOVLE2CARE platform (AccelUP) capabilities. |
| 6 | Experimentation Deployment risks due to low data availability or low stakeholder engagement | WP2 | The availability of data has already been examined, and it is anticipated to be partially resolved at the LL selection level (Phase2). Early in the project's lifecycle, EVOVLE2CARE will work with the potential stakeholders and end users to identify their needs and ways to approach and engage them. |
| 7 | Incomplete & improper project development due to the size and complexity of use-case tests | WP1 | All of the members of EVOVLE2CARE have extensive backgrounds in both large-scale research initiatives and the development and functional synchronization of extremely complex systems. Early on in the project, the Action Plan will be made accessible, concretizing the use cases and architectural design of AccelUP in a logical and practically viable way (WP1). |
| 8 | Experimentations do not reach the desired impact metrics. | WP3 | AUTH and AV will regularly revise the achievement of the innovators to avoid deviations. Major issues will be communicated to the coordination team to propose alternative actions to effectively achieve what they commit. |
| 9 | Feedback is not used to improve the services | WP3, WP4 | Feedback channels will be watched to make sure no action is left behind. We'll offer and carry out a framework for consortium and target audiences awareness. |
| 10 | Services are not utilised by the innovators in order to conduct experiments in Living Labs | WP3, WP4 | The list of services offered by LLs is not all-inclusive. The consortium has promised to be flexible and to add or remove services in response to the requests or needs of the innovators by engaging more LLs that provide complementary services. |
| 11 | Low engagement with the innovators | WP4, WP2 | Partners have a network of potential innovators at their disposal, and customized tools and messages will be developed for each target stakeholder and region. |
| 12 | Low engagement of stakeholders | WP3, WP4, WP1 | Partners already have a network of prospective stakeholders, and each target stakeholder, such as investors or corporations, will have unique tools and messages. |

1 EXCELLENCE
In the rapidly evolving landscape of healthcare, the need for innovative and effective solutions has never been more
pressing. As the sector grapples with challenges ranging from an aging population to the increasing complexity of
medical conditions, there is a growing recognition of the importance of experimentation practices. These practices,
rooted in evidence-based methodologies, offer a pathway to not only understand but also address the multifaceted
issues facing healthcare today. Central to this paradigm shift is the concept of 'living labs' - real-world environments
where users and stakeholders are actively involved and eventually lead innovations. Living labs (LLs), with their
emphasis on user-centric design and iterative testing, have emerged as a key resource for the development and
validation of innovative products, services, and solutions, particularly in the realm of HealthTech. Encompassing a
range of technological innovations from wearable devices to telemedicine platforms, HealthTech has revolutionized
the way care is delivered and received across the healthcare spectrum, becoming an enabler and a lever to assist the
full range of care-provision. This is particularly true in transitional care, which focuses on maintaining continuity as
patients move between different stages and settings of care and relies upon a vast array of data and information
gathered and exchanged between devices, systems, and word-of-mouth. Experimentation in transitional care is
indispensablde for developing specific technologies or innovative solutions that address the intricate and multifaceted
nature of healthcare transitions. By subjecting these technologies to real-world scenarios, experimentation uncovers
2 | 26

HORIZON-EIE-2023-CONNECT-02-01 STIMULATING EXPERIMENTATION PRACTICES
Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3508396 - 15/05/2024
complexities, ethical considerations, interoperability challenges, and unforeseen barriers that controlled
environments may not reveal. This process enables iterative refinement, customization, and validation of solutions
to match diverse patient needs, ensure regulatory compliance, and address stakeholder concerns.
EVOLVE2CARE delves into the intricacies of experimentation practices frameworks, with a keen focus on
standardizing and enhancing the symbiotic relationship between living labs and HealthTech in the context of
transitional care. Through EVOLVE2CARE, we aim to create an ecosystem and a framework meticulously
designed to facilitate, nurture, and amplify opportunities for experimentation, leading not only to valuable insights
but also the flourishing of novel ideas and advancements for innovators, researchers, regulators, and other pertinent
stakeholders. By developing a framework that provides a common approach for practices, EVOLVE2CARE will
provide Living Labs with a market-centric paradigm and tool that can expand the scope and value of their reach as
experimentation spaces, breaking down the silos which can occur amongst small local or regional networks. Charting
a course for a more responsive, resilient, and patient-centric healthcare future, depends on continuous innovation and
delivery of innovation to the market. Experimentation in the context of transitional care serves as a critical bridge
between conceptual ideas and practical implementation, by facing key challenges in the domain:
When assessments vary across different healthcare settings, such as hospitals, rehabilitation centres, and home care,
there is a risk of information gaps and miscommunication hindering the patient’s transition and rehabilitation
progress. Living labs offer a dynamic and controlled environment for testing, refining, and optimizing HealthTech
solutions' integration with existing healthcare systems and electronic health records (EHRs). Through this these
collaborative partnerships, Living Labs facilitate the dialogue within the stakeholder chain, allowing innovators to
uncover technological and/or systemic roadblocks to interoperability, and build more technically robust and
harmonized solutions.
In Living Lab environments standardized assessment tools and protocols can be tested with a diverse range of
stakeholders, helping HealthTech providers benchmark their performance against industry and regulatory standards.
This collaborative approach among healthcare providers, policymakers, researchers, and technology developers
ensure that assessment practices align with the goals of transitional care and are consistent across the ecosystem.
Living Labs can offer a real-life experimentation environment where both patients and caregivers can be actively
engaged in the care process in a trustful environment, ensuring they understand the assessment data, treatment options
and care plans, and that any health devices are accessible and easy to use. This requires empowering populations to
self-manage their rehabilitation journey through health innovations, improve well-being and attenuate health resource
burden.
Standardization can provide a common framework for compliance and help streamline the regulatory process. Early
discussions based on real-life experimental results with regulatory agencies can help align HealthTech development
with regulatory expectations and reduce potential roadblocks. Multidisciplinary teams brought together in a Living
Lab environment can include professionals from engineering, regulatory affairs, quality assurance, legal, and clinical
areas to ensure a holistic approach to compliance. Strong data privacy and security measures, including encryption,
secure data storage, user authentication, and compliance with data protection regulations like GDPR can be promoted.
EVOLVE2CARE Solution
EVOLVE2CARE leverages a diverse marketplace of multiple experimentation spaces around Europe, known as
Living Labs, and their associated infrastructures and networks. The primary objective is to address delays in
development, sales, and adoption of solutions which stem from the rapid rate of innovation in HealthTech. Such
delays can be caused by market forces, or by slower responses from regulatory frameworks. This asynchonicity can
lead to uncertainty for manufacturers and delays in getting products to market. By uniting and actively engaging end-

users, decision makers, regulators, innovators, and other relevant stakeholders along the entire continuum of care,
early in the experimentation process, EVOLVE2CARE aims to practically assess the real-time implications of
regulatory and other challenges that arise during the testing of innovative HealthTech solutions. Focusing on
transitional care, EVOLVE2CARE will test innovative products for 3 pre-identified Use Cases (UCs) i.e., a)
management of hospital discharge, b) home care monitoring, c) aging population and care transitions, within the
Living Lab environments. We will provide innovators with access to certified Living Labs (based on recently
published certification criteria1), each chosen for their resources that match needs relating to prototype development
for the use cases. Pairing innovators with Living Labs enhances the relevance, feasibility, and impact of research
outcomes. Simultaneously, we will actively engage stakeholders to generate further insights that can remove barriers
to innovation, ultimately accelerating the development of improved transitional care solutions based on authentic
evidence regarding the effectiveness of new products and services. EVOLVE2CARE will be a strategic project for
the Health and Wellbeing working group of the European Network of Living Labs (ENoLL) as new services will be
designed and delivered to the large living labs ecosystem.
1.1 OBJECTIVES AND AMBITION
EVOLVE2CARE aims to design a market-centric experimentation framework that effectively utilizes Living Lab
infrastructures and networks such as end-users, practitioners, corporate entities (such as hospitals, insurers), policy
makers, investors, innovators, regulators, and other stakeholders to identify and ameliorate the constraints on
innovation & commercialization posed by issues such as regulatory or market that act as barriers or gatekeepers in
the transitional care sector.
What are the Specific Needs (SN) that triggered this project?
EVOLVE2CARE will realize its ambition from different interlinked perspectives (technological, social, economic,
financial) reflected in 4 Key Objectives (KOs). Each KO is aimed at resolving a Specific Need (SN) with a major
impact on transitional care, deriving from relevant guidelines and roadmaps, and in line with the Call’s scope. The
KOs follow the SMART principles i.e., they are: Specific – linked to SNs and deliverables; Measurable – linked
with EVOLVE2CARE expected results and outcomes; Achievable – they reflect relevant expertise of partners;
Relevant – directly linked to the scope of the topic and the work programme and the Strategic Plan2, and Time-
Related – linked with specific WP/Tasks and time bounds.
[SN1]: Development of HealthTech Evaluation and Experimentation Processes in the realm of transitional care
Without harmonized experimentation support services, there may be variations in how HealthTech innovations are
evaluated and tested. Limited proof of evidence of a technology’s efficacy and safety can impact the quality and
reliability of the technologies being introduced to the market, potentially putting patients at risk.

[SN2]: Regulatory, financial, technological and operations aspects hinder the deployment of HealthTech innovations
HealthTech innovations need to comply with various regulations and standards related to healthcare, data privacy,
security, and more. Lack of regulatory clarity creates uncertainty about how HealthTech innovations should be
governed and approved, especially when they do not fit neatly into existing regulatory frameworks. Solutions may
require substantial financial investment, and reimbursement models may not be well-suited to new HealthTech
solutions, making it difficult for companies to generate revenue from their innovations. HealthTech innovations need
to interface with existing healthcare systems and technologies, which can be challenging due to the lack of
interoperability standards. HealthTech solutions also need to seamlessly fit into the daily routines of healthcare
providers and overcome the resistance to adopting new technologies due to established practices and workflows.
Lack of coordination between healthcare providers, patients, community, regulatory authorities, and investors create
non-supportive environments for efficient patient-centred plans and reintegration. Poorly executed transitions can
potentially result in readmissions, adverse events, patients’ dissatisfaction, low quality of life or even death3.

Health Affairs, 2011.

Lack of established and standardised mechanisms for knowledge sharing, collecting feedback from users and
stakeholders and use of this feedback for iterating and improving HealthTech solutions can hinder the development

1.2 COORDINATION, SUPPORT MEASURES AND METHODOLOGY
1.2.1 EVOLVE2CARE Overall Methodology underpinning the work
Three interconnected phases underpin the foundation of the EVOLVE2CARE methodological approach.
impacted by, or can impact, the process of validating HealthTech solutions in the transitional care industry. These

Stakeholders Requirements: Stakeholders that can impact or
be impacted by outcomes of experiments include: a) Innovators
and Researchers (academics, startups & SMEs) who design and
execute experiments and processes leading to products and

large entities such as hospitals, insurers, care homes, etc.,
and public bodies) who can validate the design, purchasing and
use of new offerings in the transitional care sector leading to
improvements in customer experience; c) Investors (public and
private) whose decisions to proceed with financing are
influenced by the relative positive or negative outcomes of
experimentation; d) Regulators and Compliance Bodies who determine the design and execution of regulatory
frameworks in healthcare that govern the use or development of innovative products and services; and e) Public and
Society, who can influence public perceptions on new HealthTech offerings or regulations when ethical dilemmas,
data privacy concerns or societal implications arise

Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3508396 - 15/05/2024
Utilizing existing Living Lab infrastructure and experimentation services applying various experimentation
frameworks: EVOLVE2CARE will engage (for its demonstration activities in WP3) existing certified Living Labs,
via the ENOLL network and throughout the project. In Phase 1, we will map the standardized experimentation
support services, methods, sharable databases, and infrastructure of Health-sector LLs, based on their capacity to
develop and implement experimentation frameworks that are sensitive to the diverse interests and impacts of our
target stakeholders. As cross-disciplinary collaborative environments, Living Labs should provide access to
stakeholders from various fields such as academia, business, and the medical community who can work together to
co-create, test, and validate innovative solutions in real-world settings. LLs selected should: a) utilize user-centric
approaches ensuring that the needs, preferences, and concerns of customers and users are considered, leading to
innovations that better meet their requirements, b) allow stakeholders to observe how innovations function in
authentic environments, which is particularly beneficial for innovators, investors, decision-makers, and regulators,
c) facilitate interaction between stakeholders and the innovation being tested allowing for quick adjustments of
the experimentation framework based on feedback and changing needs, d) implement established protocols for
ethical and legal compliance ensuring that experiments are conducted in a responsible and lawful manner, e) help
demonstrate the potential impact of innovations to decision makers and regulators offering tangible outcomes,
and f) encourage a culture of learning from failures, which is essential for refining experimentation frameworks.
Capitalizing on previously funded outcomes: EVOLVE2CARE will capitalize on AccelUP, a key result derived
from a previously funded EU project VITALISE4, currently in an early “proof of concept” phase. AccelUP is
designed as a centralized marketplace platform where innovators (researchers, SMEs, startups, corporates) who
utilize LL services can find and connect with suitable Labs for running experiments. The platform is designed to
provide information about each Lab’s capabilities, expertise, past projects, and available resources, while providing
a bidding mechanism to ensure competitive pricing and service offerings between innovators and Living Labs. This
bidding function is key to fostering a market-oriented approach to the provision of experimentation services and
allowing individual Living Labs to maximize the potential of their assets and resources. As institutions, Living Labs
tend to be ‘localized’ in their approach to service provision, because they are physical spaces with distinct cohorts
(or “Panels”) of engaged stakeholders. As an online “borderless” environment, AccelUP provides a means to expand
the scope and value of those local resources belonging to each Living Lab by encouraging networking, knowledge
sharing and commercial activities that are crucial to facilitating innovation, extending the breadth of each Living
Lab’s involvement beyond their present reach. Once limited to an audience in their local or regional areas, AccelUP
provides Living Labs with the opportunity to connect with stakeholders from across the globe. The platform’s
intermediation functionality will be greatly advanced within EVOLVE2CARE, allowing stakeholders – including
policymakers, investors, advocates and more – to engage in the innovation process via sharing of best practices,
knowledge bases and resources, while encouraging an overall “network effect” amongst all members of the value
chain that can lead to growth in participation, jobs, and common development frameworks.
EVOLVE2CARE’s Marketplace of Experimentation Spaces Architecture: During Phase 1, the specifications of
AccelUP will be defined and an Action Plan will be created on how the initial conceptual framework will be expanded
to establish the set of core principles and standards that all participating Living Labs must adhere to, to participate to
a Pan-European digital experimentation marketplace. These standards shall cover ethical considerations, data
privacy, participant engagement, transparency, experimentation methods, databases, and other key aspects of
experimentation. The types of services and experimentation packages that Living Labs can offer will be standardised
i.e., service packages will include details about the scope of experiments, available resources, data collection
methods, and expected outcomes. Transparent pricing models will be established to enable informed decisions of
innovators and researchers. The process for qualifying and verifying the capabilities of Living Labs before they join
the marketplace is already conducted by ENoLL and is based on specific certification criteria. This involves
evaluating their expertise, infrastructure, track record, and adherence to established standards. Collaboration tools on
the platform that will facilitate communication, project management, and data sharing between organizations and
Living Labs will be implemented to ensure seamless interaction throughout the experimentation process. Feedback
and Reviews will be enabled so users of Living Labs’ services can engage to maintain quality standards and assist
others in selecting the right lab for their needs. A mechanism for continuous improvement within the marketplace
that regularly assesses the platform’s effectiveness, quality, and overall experience will be identified and established.
Standardized Reporting for experiment outcomes will be enabled to make it easier for organizations to compare
results across different Living Labs and derive meaningful insights. To encourage knowledge sharing and
collaboration among participating Living Labs, webinars, workshops, and conferences to share best practices, case
studies, and research findings will be planned. Finally, the advanced AccelUP framework will include a governance
structure that oversees the operations of the marketplace, enforces standards, resolves disputes, and ensures the

marketplace's integrity. The main outcome of this process will be EVOLVE2CARE’s experimentation space Action
Plan. Figure 1 depicts the exploration of stakeholder requirements and the design of EVOLVE2CARE’s
experimentation space AccelUP.
Phase 2: EVOLVE2CARE Experimentation space modules & services development (linked to KO#X, WP2):
Phase 2 includes the actual development of the AccelUP marketplace modules and services (as defined in T1.4),
which are necessary to set up the functionality of EVOLVE2CARE’s experimentation space and the matching of
innovators and researchers in the transitional care sector to use LLs services from the AccelUP marketplace.
Development of AccelUP Architectural modules: The AccelUP architectural modules encapsulate the
advancements of all the core digital tools for matching innovators with LLs with the aim to test and validate
innovations and provide mechanisms to identify and communicate the regulatory implications involved when testing
and commercializing HealthTech solutions in the transitional care sector. AccelUP will add new algorithms and
profiling techniques to match innovators and Living Labs based on their respective goals, expertise, and interests.
This personalized matching will increase the likelihood of successful collaborations. Since innovators frequently
need specialised resources, tools, equipment, or expertise only found in Living Labs, AccelUP will standardise the
disclosure of clear information about the resources each Living Lab offers, assisting innovators in making informed
decisions. By facilitating efficient partnerships, AccelUP can accelerate the experimentation process. Innovators will
be able to quickly access Living Lab facilities, test their ideas, and receive real-time feedback, leading to faster
iteration cycles. The platform will encourage knowledge exchange between innovators, regulators and other
stakeholders and Living Labs. Stakeholders will be able to learn from the Living Labs' experiences, best practices,
lessons learnt and insights, contributing to more effective experimentation and alignment of regulatory frameworks
based on real-life environment results. Additionally, by using AccelUP, innovators can increase their visibility inside
the ecosystem of the platform, luring new collaborators, investors, and partners. Collaboration between innovators
from many fields and Living Labs with various specializations will be encouraged by AccelUP. Such
interdisciplinary collaboration can provide unanticipated synergies and ground-breaking ideas. Finally, AccelUP will
compile information on the results of collaboration, examples of success, and challenges faced by innovators and
Living Labs. The analysis of this data will be published to provide insightful information for increasing collaborations
and refining relevant regulatory frameworks. The platform's success can contribute to the growth of the innovation
ecosystem, attracting innovators, LLs, and stakeholders to participate and contribute to experimentation practices.
Onboarding LLs onto the AccelUP platform: Initially, EVOLVE2CARE, will engage LLs in the Health sector
through a call for expression of interest published to all ENOLL members and beyond. LLs selected will be
onboarded to the AccelUP marketplace publishing their services, infrastructures, and access to data, following a
standardized process described in the Action Plan derived during Phase 1 (see T1.3).
Onboarding Innovators and Researchers in the transitional care sector: The call for innovators and researchers
will target transitional care solutions that are being developed by startups, established companies, research
institutions or individual entrepreneurs. Proposals will be chosen in a competitive evaluation process and successful
applicants will be matched with appropriate LLs from the AccelUP marketplace. This pairing of innovators and
Living Labs will allow EVOLVE2CARE to provide specific use-cases of regulatory impact on the development of
HealthTech products and services, by observing the process in real-time. EVOLVE2CARE will engage with the
innovators to determine what practical measures are taken to ameliorate or account for regulatory issues which arise,
including workarounds, adaptations, and modifications. This empirical data can be a valuable resource for policy
makers and innovators alike, by contributing to the current understanding and future formulations of Healthcare
regulations. To recruit a suitable pool of innovators, clear and compelling messages will be crafted, including
explanations on how LLs can accelerate innovation, provide real-world validation, and offer access to diverse user
perspectives.
Use Cases (UCs): The transitional care domain has distinct challenges that can be mitigated through the application
of innovative HealthTech solutions. Some specific Use Cases (UCs) that will be used as a starting point for
experimentation of HealthTech solutions during EVOLVE2CARE are:
UC#1: Management of Hospital discharge - Μanage medication and information transfer from hospital to the next
stage of care (e.g., rehabilitation, home, nursing home). This UC can leverage on Electronic Health Records (EHRs)
that can be used to maintain a patient's medical history, including medication lists and treatment plans as well as
Health Information Exchange systems to facilitate the secure exchange of information between healthcare
organizations. It also includes medication management applications and telehealth for post-discharge follow-ups.
Technologies utilizing machine learning and predictive analytics can be also included in this UC to identify patients
at risk of readmission and allow for timely interventions.
UC#:2 Home care monitoring - Εnable remote monitoring, data sharing, and effective communication between
patients and healthcare providers. The home care monitoring is enhanced through remote monitoring devices such
as wearables, smartwatches and IoT devices that can collect and transmit vital signs, activity data, and medication

provide functionalities for self-monitoring and symptom tracking, video conferencing for teleconsultation and access
to educational resources. The collected data can be leveraged by Artificial Intelligence applications to detect
anomalies and provide early warnings of health issues.
UC#:3 Aging Population and Care Transitions: considering mobility issues, cognitive decline, and family
involvement to create accessible and intuitive technologies. This UC will include interventions targeted at Aging

reminders and navigation as well as family engagement platforms that keep family members and informal caregivers
informed and connected to the patient. All the applications should provide intuitive and accessible user interfaces, to
ensure ease of use and minimize frustration.
Eligibility Criteria: Eligibility criteria for innovators to be selected will be a) the innovation potential, b) alignment
with LL services and cost structures, c) scalability, and d) societal impact. Application requirements and processes,
deadlines and the services offered by LLs and their corresponding costs will be defined.
Application Tools & Communication methods: Application forms, templates, and guidelines for innovators to fill
out will be developed ensuring the process is transparent, competitive, user-friendly and captures essential
information. HealthTech in transitional care is by default interdisciplinary, encompassing a wide range of
specializations and technologies with cross-sectoral relevance. Multiple marketing channels i.e., social media,
newsletters, industry forums and partnerships with various organizations i.e., EIT Health, Digital, Mobility and other
KICs, Innovation Radar, Accelerator Assembly, and relevant professional/academic societies and advocacy groups
will be utilised for outreach. Each of the EVOLVE2CARE partners has extensive networks in different relevant
stakeholder areas (innovators, investors, start-ups, corporates, etc.) that will be mobilized to recruit applicants and
spread the word. Online sessions will be organized to provide potential applicants with more insights into LL services,
the application process, and the benefits of participating. Contact information for inquiries and assistance to potential
applicants will be offered. The review and selection process will involve evaluating each application against the
predefined criteria, who will make the final decision on the most promising projects. These results will be harmonized
to account for any significant difference in the evaluation scores. The consortium will also identify and contract one
external ethics expert that will review the short-listed projects for ethics issues, require clarifications from the
applicants (if needed) and provide a written opinion on the ethics issues involved in these projects as an ethics report
submitted to the consortium identifying any potential threats or changes. This report will be submitted to the EC
during the ethics check (M16). Once the selection process is completed, the successful innovators will be announced
through various channels showcasing the LL services they will be using. Selected innovators will work closely with
EVOLVE2CARE partners to facilitate a smooth onboarding process.
Tailored training program to address the needs of LLs and innovators/researchers: Two training courses will
be designed and implemented by ENOLL and AV, targeting an extended audience as participants. The first training
program will target LLs, including those which do not participate in the subcontracting services of EVOLVE2CARE
but have an interest in increasing their capacities in supporting innovators but also learn from successes or failures.
The second training program will be tailored to the needs of innovators/researchers aimed at increasing their
capacities in making the most out of real-life experimentations environments and enhance their commercialization
potential. Both trainings will create a supportive ecosystem that fosters effective collaboration, successful
experimentation, and the potential commercialization of innovative solutions (see T2.3 &T2.4).
Testing and Validation of the matchmaking process between LLs and innovators/researchers:
EVOLVE2CARE consortium includes SPLORO, which offers existing matchmaking services between innovators,
investors, and various stakeholders through a tested and validated functional digital tool. SPLORO will provide the
functional methodology and the assessment baseline for the matchmaking process between Living Labs and
Innovators. Sploro’s methodology is currently being utilised among US, Canadian and EU research entities for the
NGI Sargasso programme5. The application of the methodology for matching innovators with LLs will give useful
insight on how to design and implement the bidding process for attracting the right LLs in a research and
experimentation project within the AccelUP platform.
Phase 3: Implementation of Experimentation Services and Space - AccelUP (linked to KO#2,3, and WP3)
Phase 3 includes the execution of the experimentation services, during which HealthTech innovations in the
transitional care sector will be tested in LL infrastructures identified in Phase 2. This phase includes the execution of
each experiment for the Use Case identified, in realistic environments as well as the monitoring and evaluation of
the experimentation process. The innovators who have been chosen and matched with a LL will be given access to
the agreed-upon resources of the particular LL. The matched LLs will be offered payment vouchers by
EVOLVE2CARE on behalf of the innovators to compensate for the experimentation services they will provide. An

1.2.2 Do not significant harm
EVOLVE2CARE is fully compliant with the Do No Significant Harm (DNSH) Principle since its activities (carried
out during the course of the project, including the use of the funding via financial support to third parties envisioned
in the project), do not significantly harm any of the six environmental objectives set out in Articles 9 and 17 of the
EU Sustainable Finance Taxonomy Regulation6.
1.2.3 Appropriateness of open science practices implementation
Given the nature of EVOLVE2CARE, a pure orientation to openly share research data or other research processes
are not a core element of this project. Coordination and support actions are not research initiatives per se.
Nevertheless, we foresee concrete measures and actions which help us to embrace the “open” movement as such, in
an aim to promote openness, transparency and the results attained by the project.
▫ Open access to publications produced in the context of the project by Innovators/Researchers: We realise
that our training programme or the outcomes of the experimentations may involve the generation of scientific

1.2.4 Research data management and management of other research outputs
Research data management is understood at two levels: (1) EVOLVE2CARE as a project and (2) the selected LLs
participating by providing their services in the experimentation Use Cases and innovators/researchers wishing to test
their solutions and participate in the training programs.
Regarding the first, the project itself is not performing activities related to research. Nevertheless, EVOLVE2CARE
will carry out data collection, including personal data and metadata (in context of the internal personal data of the
consortium partners but especially the personal data of the innovators/researcher applying to our open calls and
joining our public events). EVOLVE2CARE will perform a data management needs assessment and release a data
management plan (D5.3). All processing of personal data will be conducted in accordance with the provisions of the
GDPR. Analysis data that will be collected and/or generated by the project will be made public. In addition, and to
promote transparency and the good use of EU-funding, EVOLVE2CARE will publish an Open Dataset with the
beneficiaries of our open calls including their basic data (non-personal) and the experimentation results as a result
of their participation in the open calls and their collaborations with the LLs. This dataset will be made available at
ZENODO.
Regarding the selected experimentation instances of the use cases carried out by the innovators/researchers within
the Living Labs, the sub-contracting agreements (T3.1) will include the rules related to publication of results and
research data management by Horizon Europe. The results of the research performed by the innovators/researchers
in the context will be shared with the scientific community, commercial players, civil society, policymakers and
regulators. They must also take action to use their project results for commercial purposes, to tackle societal problems
or in policymaking (‘exploitation’).
EVOLVE2CARE will recommend the selected explorers to use the different various free-of-charge services to
support the dissemination activities such as:
▫ Open Research Europe platform: An open access, publishing platform for scientific papers for Horizon 2020 and
Horizon Europe beneficiaries, including open peer review and article revision.
▫ Horizon Results platform: A platform for showcasing research results, finding collaboration opportunities and
getting inspired by the results of others.
▫ Horizon Results Booster: Free consulting services including a portfolio dissemination and exploitation strategy,
business plan development and go-to-market support.
▫ Innovation Radar: An initiative that identifies high-potential innovations, based on a data-driven methodology,
and assists EU-funded researchers and innovators in reaching the market with their innovation.

| Increasing knowledge on experimentation frameworks for testing innovations in the realm of healthcare necessitates a holistic strategy with clear objectives. We begin by pinpointing the specific challenges in transitional care and the potential of HealthTech to address them, while also identifying gaps in current regulatory frameworks. A collaborative approach is the cornerstone of this endeavour, combining focused intelligence gathering from all stakeholder groups with initial findings from Living Labs own experience. From this first stage we move to strengthen partnerships between innovators, regulators, healthcare providers, and patients, in order to collectively research and test new HealthTech solutions in real-world settings. Living Labs, with their diverse environments and demographics, serve as the ideal platform for such collaborative endeavours. The overall scheme of our project presents a clear step-by-step process to make this happen, from a requisites’ gathering in phase 1, to training programs and the development of experimentation services in Phase 2, to an actual experimentation suite in Phase 3 that will significantly enable us to increase knowledge on experimentation frameworks for testing innovations under the transitional care domain in LLs’ real-life environments. |  |  |

| In essence, by combining targeted training with the tangible benefits of Living Labs, EVOLVE2CARE ensures that innovative solutions are not only developed but are also ready for deployment and commercialisation in the real world. This holistic approach significantly increases the chances of success and market adoption for these innovations. The path to deploying and commercialising new innovative solutions is multifaceted, and EVOLVE2CARE has strategically positioned itself to streamline this journey. One of the primary avenues is through our tailored training programs. In tasks like T2.3 and T2.4, we've designed comprehensive training modules for both Living Labs (LLs) and innovators/researchers. These programs delve into essential topics such as understanding innovation ecosystems, best practices in LL operations, effective collaboration skills, and even sector-specific training. For innovators, the training emphasizes the role of LLs, along with design thinking, prototyping, business model development, finances, investment, and pitching. By equipping both LLs and innovators with these skills, we ensure that the innovations are not only technically sound but also closer to market- readiness. Access to Living Labs is another cornerstone of our strategy. Living Labs, as highlighted in Phase 1, offer a unique environment where innovations can be tested, refined, and validated in real-world settings. This hands-on approach ensures that the solutions are not only theoretically viable but also practically impactful. By allowing innovators to engage with real users, gather feedback, and iterate in real-time, we drastically reduce the time and resources typically required to refine a product or service. |  |  |

| Within the framework of the project, we have chosen to concentrate on transitional care as a sub-sector of the health field. Specifically, we will focus on innovative solutions as Use Cases (UCs) in three areas: a) hospital discharge management, b) home care monitoring, and c) aging population and care transitions. These areas, inherently intertwined with multiple industries like technology, pharmaceuticals, and finance, present unique challenges and opportunities, as innovations span a wide range, from wearable health tech devices to sophisticated telemedicine platforms, and from AI-driven diagnostic tools to IoT-enabled patient monitoring systems. Recognizing the intricate nature of these sectors, we emphasize the importance of a coordinated approach between innovators and regulators. The emerging solutions in transitional care promise not only improved patient outcomes and streamlined processes but also bring forth challenges related to data privacy, ethical considerations, and standardization. The iterative experimentation process that will be performed in the LLs provides an opportunity to study in real time the regulatory hurdles or enablers that innovators face as they develop new products and services. Our aim is to work collaboratively with regulators and encourage them to engage early in the validation process along with innovators and users. This can ensure that the solutions not only meet but exceed the highest standards of care, safety, and compliance, and that regulations and policy decisions reflect the complexity of human |  |  |

|  | Identify legal, regulatory, fiscal, technical, and operational pre-requisites or barriers for different use-cases |  |
|  | which hamper the development of innovation; |  |
| To effectively identify the myriad of legal, regulatory, fiscal, technical, and operational prerequisites or barriers that obstruct innovation, we employ a two-pronged strategy. Firstly, we conduct a rigorous task-driven analysis, focusing on our three Use Cases (a) hospital discharge management, b) home care monitoring, and c) aging population and care transitions). This analysis, under the umbrella of "Drivers and Barriers for the development of Healthtech innovations", provides a holistic view of the current challenges, revealing the intricate factors that might deter innovators from realizing their full potential in each area. Secondly, our methodology emphasizes direct engagement with stakeholders. By interacting with a spectrum of participants, from innovators and researchers to regulators and the general public, we glean first hand insights into the real-world manifestations of these barriers. This feedback, coupled with our observations from Living Labs, offers a unique vantage point, enabling us to understand and subsequently address the multifaceted challenges that impede the growth of innovation. |  |  |
|  | Target group: Innovators and entrepreneurs, policy makers, industry experts, investors |  |
| Scale: Interact with at least 20 different organisations from at least 10 different countries to provide information and feedback regarding pre-requisites, barriers or enablers to innovative products and services in the 3 UCs. |  |  |
|  | Significance: Soliciting feedback from across the EU and engaging with an increased number of external |  |
|  | stakeholders will provide a wide perspective of knowledge to base our conclusions upon. |  |

### Table 3

| Column 1 | EXPECTED OUTCOME #7 | Column 3 |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | Explore areas where the European supervisory authority exists and pan-European testing might be |  |
|  | desirable. |  |
| We can approach this two-fold: (1) Identification and Analysis: Leveraging our expertise in health, transitional care, and multi-sectoral innovations, we'll delve deep into the European landscape to pinpoint sectors overseen by a European supervisory authority. Our approach will be data-driven, utilizing insights from our previous tasks and methodologies to discern areas where pan-European regulations are prevalent or necessary. By understanding the intricacies of these sectors, especially in health and transitional care, we can identify where pan-European testing is not just desirable but essential for innovation and progress. (2) Engagement and Collaboration: Building on our commitment to knowledge sharing and stakeholder collaboration, we'll initiate dialogues with member states, industry leaders, and the European supervisory authority. Through workshops and forums, we'll discuss the potential of pan-European testing in the identified areas, drawing from real-life examples and use-cases from our tasks. By fostering this collaborative environment, we aim to bridge the gap between innovators and regulators, ensuring that pan-European testing aligns with the needs of the industry while upholding regulatory standards. |  |  |
|  | Target group: Supervisory authorities, industry, member state representatives |  |
| Scale: Reaching at least 5 supervisory authorities from 10 different MS expanding to at least 20 in the second year. |  |  |
|  | Significance: Covering 20 countries out of the 27 in the EU and possibly expanding to associated member states |  |
|  | will provide us full coverage to maximise our impact in regulatory alignment. |  |

HORIZON-EIE-2023-CONNECT-02-01 STIMULATING EXPERIMENTATION PRACTICES
Associated with document Ref. Ares(2024)3508396 - 15/05/2024
In response to the overarching vision of fostering interconnected, inclusive, and efficient innovation ecosystems
across the EU, our project resonates deeply with this tripartite mission: 1. Interconnected: At the heart of our
initiative lies the principle of interconnectedness, with HealthTech serving as a pivotal enabler. By facilitating the
digital innovation of the healthcare sector, we are not only streamlining processes but also ensuring that
information, resources, and innovations are seamlessly shared across regions. This interconnected approach
ensures that best practices, technological advancements, and patient-centric solutions are universally accessible,
bridging gaps and fostering a cohesive healthcare landscape across the EU. 2. Inclusive: Our emphasis on
transitional care is a testament to our commitment to inclusivity. Recognizing that healthcare is not just about
treating ailments but also about ensuring a smooth transition for patients and their families, our project adopts a
holistic approach. By focusing on transitional care, we are ensuring that patients, their families, and carers are at
the center of our innovation strategy. This inclusive approach ensures that the unique needs, challenges, and
aspirations of these stakeholders are addressed, fostering a healthcare ecosystem that is truly patient-centric and
inclusive of all. 3. Efficient Innovation: Efficiency is the linchpin of our innovation strategy, and our use of
Living Labs (LLs) underscores this commitment. By leveraging LLs as the foundational platform for
experimentation, we are ensuring that innovations are not only cutting-edge but also grounded in real-world
applicability. The hands-on, practical nature of LLs ensures that solutions are tested, refined, and validated in real-
world settings, ensuring that they are not only technically sound but also practically impactful. This approach
drastically reduces the time-to-market, ensures relevance, and guarantees that our innovations are both efficient
and effective. In conclusion, our project is not just aligned with the EU's vision but actively embodies it. Through
our interconnected approach with HealthTech, our inclusive focus on transitional care, and our commitment to
efficient innovation via Living Labs, we are poised to make significant strides in advancing the European Research
Area and the New European Innovation Agenda.

| In response to the vision of enhancing cross-border network connectivity and fostering inter-regional collaboration among regional innovation valleys, our approach is rooted in the power of Living Labs (LLs) and their potential to revolutionize transitional care across Europe. By leveraging the power of a marketplace platform, AccelUP, we provide a centralized yet global space for stakeholders of all measure to locate, learn about, and collaborate with Living Labs and each other. This platform has the potential to greatly enhance the entire value chain of HealthTech innovation, while maximizing the spread of knowledge and use of resources. Living Labs contain a wealth of tools, machinery, technology, and know-how which is often only deployed on a local level. Our centralized ecosystem approach can unlock the value of these resources to a vastly wider group of beneficiaries, thus extending the power of Living Labs far beyond their individual geographical locations. Digitizing the availability of these services allows for greener innovation processes by lowering the time and energy required for locating a suitable Living Lab, and reducing the duplication of resources when new, siloed Living Labs are created. |  |  |

2.2.1.2 Exploitation
The exploitation of the project refers to (1) the project itself, and (2) the experimentation results by the supported
innovators within the programme.
(1) The first case provides for the creation of two different lines to further exploit and continue the EVOLVE2CARE
initiative beyond the project end. The tailored training programs is a key outcome and result which can be further
replicated as a “franchised” programme, oriented to facilitate access of deeptech innovators in experimentation spaces
where they can accelerate the iteration of experimentation cycles and get real-life feedback. Another important
outcome that will be the subject of a collaborative exploitation plan is the AccelUP Platform, which will be further
developed and verified inside EVOLVE2CARE. Launching a Joint Venture focused to the commercialization of
AccelUP experimentation space platform is intended to prepare the way for the go-to-market. The first business
development activities will be carried out to spur market demand for the technology, also leveraging on the
stakeholders involved during EVOLVE2CARE. 12 – 24 months after the project completion -> Early Customers:
The commercialisation strategy will be implemented in the EU and worldwide, leveraging on AccelUP onboarding
mechanism from various distribution channels. (2) EVOVLE2CARE will assist innovators/researchers in setting up
an effective commercialization strategy along with helping them in IP matters and regulatory compliance issues. The
connection with other experimentation spaces will be also be further investigated.
2.2.2 Strategy for the management of intellectual property
The nature of the project, CSA, is not a traditional research project where results are due to exhaustive IPR issues
which usually need deep analysis in the context of the project. The consortium will create a Consortium Agreement
(CA) based on standard DESCA model and will describe all partners’ project related background information and
IPR they make available to the project. The CA will be signed by all the project participants before the start of the
project. The basic principle is that knowledge resulting from the project belongs to the project partner who
generated it. If knowledge is generated jointly and separate parts cannot be distinguished, it will be jointly owned,
unless the contractors concerned agree on a different solution.

HORIZON-EIE-2023-CONNECT-02-01: STIMULATING EXPERIMENTATION PRACTICES
3 QUALITY AND EFFICIENCY OF THE IMPLEMENTATION
3.1 WORK PLAN AND RESOURCES
3.1.1 Brief presentation of the overall structure of the work plan
WP1: EVOLVE2CARE experimentation space framework. This WP will concentrate on describing the particular
needs of the many stakeholders who may be influenced or affected by the results of experiments. Throughout the
project, EVOLVE2CARE will rely on already-established and certified Living Labs, and capitalize on AccelUP
(currently in proof of concept phase), a centralised experimentation platform where innovators (researchers, SMEs,
start-ups, and corporates) can find and connect with appropriate LL services. An Action Plan will set the
specifications of the digital modules to be advanced
WP2: Matching AccelUP Experimentation Space and Services with Innovators and Researchers. This WP
entails creating the digital AccelUP marketplace modules required to build up the EVOLVE2CARE experimentation
marketplace in accordance with the requirements and services specified in T1.4. It also encapsulates all the core
architectural modules for testing the regulatory implications involved in developing and commercializing innovative
solutions in the transitional care sector. This WP also includes specialised training for LLs to help them improve their
abilities to assist innovators and researchers, and for innovators and researchers to effectively leverage LLs for their
innovation projects.
WP3: Set Up of AccelUP experimentation Space and Services Execution, Lessons Learnt and Best practices.
This WP includes the onboarding process of innovators and researchers onto the AccelUP platform and the execution
of the experimentations, during which innovations in the transitional care sector will be tested in the pre-defined LL
infrastructures. The Use Case experiments will be conducted in realistic environments and will be followed by the
monitoring and evaluation of each experiment.
WP4: EVOLVE2CARE’s People-Centric Experimentation Ecosystem Outreach, Sustainability and
Clustering. WP4 includes the Dissemination, Communication and Exploitation strategy, activities and materials.
Finally, WP5: Project Coordination and Management deals with the coordination and management of the overall
project implementation.

3.2 CAPACITY OF PARTICIPANTS AND CONSORTIUM AS A WHOLE
3.2.1 Consortium description
EVOLVE2CARE consortium is composed of five
outstanding partners from five EU countries
(Greece, Bulgaria, Belgium, Spain, Cyprus). AUTH
and particularly iMedPhys that undertakes the
execution of this project, is the expert organization
that has led the design and implementation of
ACELLUP in the framework of the VITALISE
project (GA# 101007990). AUTH has established a
LL, ThessAHALL, that is currently a user-centered
innovation and experimentation hub in Central
Macedonia, Greece. AUTH has long expertise in
European project execution and coordination with a
portfolio of more than 70 projects (national and EU).
ENOLL (BE) is an international, non-profit,
independent association of benchmarked Living
Labs offering an enlarged network of collaborative
testing and experimentation environments, where
stakeholders from the quadruple helix i.e., citizens, Figure 3 EVOLVE2CARE consortium and reach
government, industry, and academia work together
to co-create, test, and evaluate innovative solutions in real-world settings. Its aim is to promote the Living Labs
concept to influence EU policies, enhance Living Labs services and enable their implementation at a global
level. Living Labs provide a unique opportunity to develop and implement experimentation frameworks that are
sensitive to the diverse interests and impacts of diverse stakeholders.
ANTHOLOGY VENTURES (BG) is a start-up and business angel studio with a large experience in promoting the
commercialization and investment of innovative ideas and research results. AV acts as operational co-founders for

3.2.1.1 Coordinator
Dr. Evdokimos Konstantinidis has over 15 years of work experience in EU policies and
projects in the R&D field. He is the leader of the Assistive Technologies and Silver Science
Research Group in the Medical Physics and Digital Innovation Lab, Aristotle University of
Thessaloniki and he has experience in coordinating large European projects such as: i) the
Research Infrastructure H2020 project, VITALISE - aiming to harmonize the procedures and
ICT tools of the Health and Wellbeing Living Labs towards creating an open ecosystem for the
European researchers and ii) the RAISE Horizon Europe funded project on services for the
European Open Science Cloud, towards a crowdsource network for data processing. He is the President of European
Network of Living Labs (ENoLL) and coordinator of the Health and Wellbeing Living Labs Working Group. In
2020, as a result of the H2020 funded project named CAPTAIN H2020 (technically coordinated by him), he co-
founded CAPTAIN-COACH, one of the first 10 spin-offs of AUTH. He has been principal investigator for a couple
of national and international funded projects and has authored more than 140 publications in various international
peer-reviewed journals and conferences (h-index: 18, ~ 2259 citations).

3.2.1.3 Ethics Advisory Board
The EVOLVE2CARE consortium will also establish an Ethics Advisory Board that will consist of at least 50% external
advisors. The external advisors will be identified by the consortium and a formal invitation for participation will be
sent to them by the Coordinator. Their role will be to meet with the consortium every six months and provide guidance
on ethics issues. All the required documents will be provided to them beforehand. The consortium will also
investigate if a Non-Disclosure Agreement has to be signed between the external advisors and the consortium.
3.2.2 Complementarity and contributions of the partners
The partners in the consortium are perfectly fit for a programme of this nature that aims to stimulate experimentation
practices. The coordinator, AUTH with its experience in leading the activities of developing the AccelUP platform
in VITALISE has a pivotal role in the advancement and exploitation of the AccelUP for matching innovators with
Living Labs holding great potential for fostering innovation. AUTH developing AccelUP as conceptualized in
EVOLVE2CARE, and based on its experience a Living Lab. In the health sector, will advance AccelUP to a
centralized platform that streamlines the process of connecting innovators with suitable Living Labs. This efficiency
can accelerate the collaboration process and reduce barriers for both parties. AccelUP can showcase a variety of
Living Labs, each with its own unique focus and specialization. This diversity of options will allow innovators from
various domains to find Living Labs that align with their experimental needs. Finally, AUTH’s experience from the
Centrifuge & Rehabilitation Living Lab provides a strong foundation for leading innovation activities within the
project's defined Use Cases, ensuring that the experimentation is guided by relevant knowledge and insights that can
be used and capitalized also in other disciplines. AUTH can facilitate the transfer of knowledge and insights from
the Centrifuge & Rehabilitation Living Lab to the new project's teams and collaborators. This knowledge transfer
can accelerate the learning curve and ensure that valuable expertise is disseminated.
European Network of Living Labs (ENOLL), brings together a vast network of member organizations (historically
480+ members) offering experimentation environments around EU and globally. With its years of experience,
ENOLL, has developed a deep understanding of how to create, manage, and optimize such experimentation spaces.
ENOLL promotes a collaborative approach to innovation bringing together various stakeholders, including citizens,
researchers, businesses, and governments, fostering a diverse and multidisciplinary environment that is essential for
successful experimentation and innovation. Its wide network includes universities, research institutions, companies,
and public bodies. This wide-reaching network enables ENOLL to connect the EVOLVE2CARE initiatives with a
rich pool of resources, expertise, and potential beneficiaries. ENOLL also acts as a platform for sharing best practices
and lessons learned from various living lab projects. This knowledge-sharing aspect can greatly benefit a
EVOLVE2CARE promoting experimentation practices, as it provides insights into successful strategies, challenges,
and innovative approaches that have worked in different contexts. ENOLL has been active in advocating for policies
that support open innovation and experimentation. Their involvement in policy discussions at local, regional, and
national levels can help create a favorable environment for testing innovations and ensure alignment with broader
regulatory and innovation goals. ENOLL's focus on living labs is not limited to technology-driven projects. They
emphasize social innovation, sustainability, and community engagement. This holistic approach ensures that the
EVOLVE2CARE goals align with broader societal needs and trends. ENOLL finally provides training, workshops,
and capacity-building activities to its members. For the EVOLVE2CARE which aims to stimulate experimentation
practices, this means access to resources and knowledge that can enhance the effectiveness and impact of the project.
ANTHOLOGY VENTURES (AV) has a track record of nurturing startups and Business Angels and understands
the challenges and opportunities associated with experimentation, rapid iteration, and scaling new ideas. This
experience can be directly applied to EVOLVE2CARE, ensuring effective implementation of the experimentation
practices i.e., the Use Cases. AV through its bespoke accelerator, “the Clinic”, a 60-day mentoring and coaching
program emphasize at rapid and iterative approach to building and refining new concepts. This aligns well with the
experimentation mindset, where ideas are tested, adjusted, and improved based on real-world feedback. Anthology
Ventures can help structure the project's experimentation process for maximum learning and adaptation. AV is
experienced in providing start-ups with resources such as funding, mentorship, workspace, and access to networks.
For a project focused on experimentation practices, these resources can facilitate the creation and testing of

prototypes, concepts, and innovative solutions, enhancing the EVOLVE2CARE's overall impact. AV is also
accustomed to managing and mitigating risks associated with innovation. It understands that not all experiments will
succeed, and they can help design the Use Cases in a way that allows for controlled risk-taking while minimizing
potential negative impacts. AV has an established network within the startup ecosystem, including investors, industry
experts, and potential collaborators. These connections can be leveraged to bring in additional expertise, funding,
and partnerships that are crucial for the success of EVOLVE2CARE stimulating experimentation practices and
fostering innovation. Finally, AV’s experience on building startups that have the potential to scale rapidly can
translate well to the EVOLVE2CARE’s scalability goals, as successful experimentation practices can lead to scalable

SPLORO, with its validated methodology of linking innovators to financiers can play a significant role in supporting
EVOLVE2CARE for stimulating experimentation practices. It will help in developing EVOLVE2CARE
methodology for matching innovators with Living Labs and their experimentation spaces. SPLORO’s methodology
will act as a bridge between innovators with experimental ideas and experimentation spaces that provide real-world
testing environments and a network of diverse stakeholders. This methodology will be incorporated into AccelUP to
facilitate the identification and connection of suitable innovators with the right Living Labs, fostering a productive
collaboration. Its experience in Cascade funding which involves allocating financial resources to innovative projects
through a tiered approach can also leverage funding opportunities for the innovations being tested in the Living Lab
environments. Funding opportunities through cascade funding can be identified at various stages of experimentation,
allowing innovators to develop and refine their concepts within the Living Lab environment. These opportunities for
linking innovators with resources that can support the prototype development, testing, and scaling efforts can
accelerate the experimentation process by providing innovators with the resources needed to carry out their
experiments more effectively and efficiently. This can lead to faster iteration cycles, quicker validation of ideas, and
accelerated learning and therefor a greater EVOLVE2CARE impact beyond the project.
VILABS is an SME created in 2011, that acts both as a private research and innovation laboratory and as an
innovation Hub for startups. VIL provides a wide range of research, development and consulting services to national
as well as international enterprises and organisations, utilising a unique set of tangible and intangible resources,
including knowledge, facilities, human and financial resources, supporting researchers and entrepreneurs to innovate.
VIL is active in the sectors of ICT, cybersecurity, energy, health, gender and entrepreneurship through all stages of
Research, Technological Development and Innovation. VIL has become a leading promoter of linkages between
research and/or educational centers, organisations, public and private sector and the industrial world. It has
coordinated and provided expert support on EU projects providing high impact and good practices into various
concepts and domains.
3.2.3 Industrial / Commercial involvement to ensure exploitation
The exploitation of results needs to be seen at three levels (1) researchers/innovators: the aim of the training and
services provision (2) consortium: Improving their connection with innovators i.e., including deeptech startups as
the next “big thing” in the investment arena (3) AccelUP experimentation space: the experimentation space will
promote experimentation environments around EU which are conducive to innovation. The five partners are
committed to building a sustainable product which can be extended and capitalized further after.
4 ETHICS SELF-ASSESSMENT
4.1 ETHICAL DIMENSION OF THE OBJECTIVES, METHODOLOGY AND LIKELY IMPACT
EVOLVE2CARE is not a research project in the strict sense. We are a CSA proposal which will provide support
services to innovators/ researchers to decrease barriers to innovation. Regarding ethical aspects of the proposal, it is
subject to the Horizon Europe Ethics Review Procedure’s Ethics Screening process. At all times throughout the
project the consortium will remain fully aware of, and compliant with, the ethical and legal principles and
requirements of European Union policy: http://ec.europa.eu/research/swafs/index.cfm?pg=policy&lib=ethics
4.2 COMPLIANCE WITH ETHICAL PRINCIPLES AND RELEVANT LEGISLATIONS
EVOLVE2CARE is committed to perform the project in compliance with the General Data Protection Regulation
2016/679 ("GDPR") and any implementing local legislation (collectively referred to as the "EU Data Protection
Legislation"). All details about personal data collection, processing, storage and destruction will be specified in the
Data Management Plan of the project (D5.1), and specific measures about privacy and protection of personal data
will be carried out in the scope of different tasks. One of the main objectives of EVOLVE2CARE project is to
identify, catalogue and make it available existing data sources and datasets, including among others, personal data.
The project will respect specific existing conditions about access, permissions, etc. to use of all datasets in general
and personal datasets in particular. The following aspects, among others, will be considered:
25 | 26

HORIZON-EIE-2023-CONNECT-02-01 STIMULATING EXPERIMENTATION PRACTICES

-Identify which personal data are going to be processed, develop a proportionality test in order to verify whether all
those data are needed to successfully contact LLs and innovators/researchers to participate in the project, set-up the
